So until the first system call (I$Open <= (a0)="/t2" d0.b=3) you have device accesses to the PIC (68070), UART (68070), DMA (68070), SLAVE and VDSC
(I do not know what device is at $318000, ignore those writes, the same can be done for the DMA as it's just initialization).

After that point the boot code is done, every device access after that is from a legitimate CD-RTOS driver and tagged with the name of that driver. 

The very high instruction counts on some RD.X operations indicate polling until a timeout (initial USR) or some event (e.g. vertical sync on CSR1R).
The full trace shows the actual values read/written so that should give you some idea how to "minimally" implement emulation with this specific ROM.

However, if you're going for "clean" OS-9 emulation I would just skip the boot code at all.
Just find the kernel module and start its entry point after minimal memory and register setup (the requirements should be documented somewhere in the Microware documentation and are probably also in The OS-9 Guru).
